Shipwreck records 1901-2026 | EMODnet Heritage Ship Wrecks
Page 1
 Shipwreck Records: 1901-2026
 2,677 source-recorded wreck entries
 
Source: EMODnet Human Activities - Heritage Ship Wrecks WFS. The source combines records from public maritime-heritage datasets including SHOM, Historic England, Ireland's National Monuments
Service, Lloyd's List and Wrecksite-derived records. Fields shown: loss date, time, vessel name, vessel type, country/source area, circumstances, and WGS84 latitude/longitude.
Important: YYYY-??-?? means the source supplies only the year. ‘unknown’ means the source does not publish that field. Coordinates describe the reported wreck-site point and may differ from the vessel's
exact position at the moment of loss. The data include identified and unidentified wrecks; not every record establishes fatalities.

LE PIONNIER
Cargo, commercial
vessel
France
On 02/15/1902, after setting sail from the port of St-Tropez to go to Marseille,
she ran aground in the middle of the night around 11 p.m., due to violent
squalls. The calcium carbide contained in its bunkers, on contact with water,
explodes
43.17116086, 6.64692324

EDOUARD B. WINSLOW
Schooner
France
On 07/10/1917, the six-masted ship, coming from the United States, was in
sight of the French coast and was about to enter the Loire, when an explosion
followed by a fire forced her to run aground in front of Pornichet where she
burned for two days. The

SILJA
Tanker, oil tanker, cargo
ship
France
The tanker SILJA left Genoa on 07/23/1969 for the Persian Gulf. On
07/24/1969 at 03:15 the VILLE DE MAJUNGA (cargo of 10500t) leaving
Marseilles collided with the SILJA. The SILJA, empty but not degassed,
exploded and sank forward in 2 to 3 minutes. Bala
42.65111326, 6.28791644
